    ТОЛЬКО УЛУЯШЕНИЕМ ГРАФИКИ СУБМИРА И МАКРОМИРА - минимально универсально     
                                                                                
    элегантно везде где точно 100% можешь улучшить то улучшай - шейдеры         
  например                                                                      
    динамические тени воду освещение отражения текстуры эффекты свечения        
  партиклы                                                                      
    следы крови/снарядов/взрывов магические эффекты и тд - старайся работать    
                                                                                
    макс системно и широко без мелкого хардкода (стиль игры фентези пиксль аля

⏺ Now I have the full picture. 2 frames in flight means the light SSBO needs
  per-frame double-buffering to avoid a write-after-read hazard. The existing
  vk_buffer only exposes device-local + staged update. For a tiny (~1KB)
  per-frame SSBO, a host-visible persistently-mapped ring is the correct,
  stall-free approach. Let me read vk_buffer.cpp to see what host-visible
  helpers exist and how update is implemented.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The existing VulkanBuffer::update calls vkQueueWaitIdle every call — a full
  GPU stall, forbidden per-frame by the no-stall rule. So the light SSBO must be
  a host-visible, persistently-mapped, per-frame-in-flight ring (2 buffers + 2
  descriptor sets), written after the fence wait — the standard hazard-free
  pattern. This keeps the plan's "set 0, binding 1" exactly (binding 0 = shadow
  image, binding 1 = per-frame SSBO in the same set).

⏺ I now have the complete call chain. Design decision for Inc 1:

  - Set 0 gains binding 1 = light SSBO (STORAGE_BUFFER, FRAGMENT). Since the
  buffer is written per-frame and there are 2 frames in flight, shadowSet_
  becomes shadowSet_[2] — each set has the same shadow sampler at binding 0 but
  its own per-frame light buffer at binding 1.
  - Add a create_host_mapped helper to VulkanBuffer (persistently-mapped
  HOST_COHERENT) — the existing update() stalls, so it can't be used per-frame.
  - Thread frameIndex through record_main only (record_shadow doesn't bind
  set 0).
  - Inc 1 does NOT touch shaders or gpu_smoke3d — the shaders don't reference
  binding 1 yet, so the harness stays valid. That isolates plumbing risk from
  shader risk. The frame must be pixel-identical to baseline.
  
  Let me confirm the .cpp includes and constants before editing.

⏺ The fence discipline confirms the write is safe: acquire_frame waits on and
  resets inFlight[currentFrame] (vk_renderer.cpp:235,257), so once it returns,
  frame currentFrame's resources are GPU-idle — writing
  lightBuf_[currentFrame].mapped in record_main (which runs after acquire) needs
  no stall. That's the no-stall transfer rule satisfied.

      33      return base * (ambient + sunColor * sunTerm * shadow);
      34  }
      35  
      36 +// -------------------------------------------------------------
         +--------------                                                  
      37 +// POSITIONAL (point) lights — the universal second half of subw
         +orld lighting.                                                  
      38 +//                                                              
      39 +// A single fragment-visible storage buffer at set 0 / binding 1
         + holds every                                                    
      40 +// active point light for the frame (torches, lit windows, spell
         + / projectile                                                   
      41 +// glows, the player's own light). It lives on the SAME shared d
         +escriptor set as                                                
      42 +// the shadow map, so ONE descriptor lights all five lit passes 
         +— mirroring how                                                 
      43 +// the directional sun/moon was unified into one lit_surface(). 
         +The C++ side                                                    
      44 +// (src/sub/lighting.h GpuLightBuffer) owns the matching std430 
         +layout:                                                         
      45 +//   count : u32, then 12 bytes pad → 16-byte header,           
      46 +//   lights[]: vec4 posRadius (xyz = world pos, w = radius metre
         +s),                                                             
      47 +//             vec4 colorRgbInt (rgb = linear colour, w = intens
         +ity gain).                                                      
      48 +// The array is runtime-sized here and fixed (kSubworldMaxLights
         +) on the C++                                                    
      49 +// side; the loop is clamped so a corrupt count can never run aw
         +ay.                                                             
      50 +//                                                              
      51 +// Contract: this term is ADDITIVE on top of lit_surface() and i
         +s unshadowed by                                                 
      52 +// the sun map (a torch is not occluded by the sun's shadow). Wh
         +en the buffer's                                                 
      53 +// count is 0 — the state until an emitter is gathered (Inc 3+) 
         +— point_lights()                                                
      54 +// returns (0,0,0), so wiring it in is provably inert until real
         + lights exist.                                                  
      55 +#define TIMAERT_MAX_POINT_LIGHTS 32                             
      56 +                                                                
      57 +struct GpuPointLight {                                          
      58 +    vec4 posRadius;   // xyz world position, w radius (metres)  
      59 +    vec4 colorRgbInt; // rgb colour, w intensity gain           
      60 +};                                                              
      61 +                                                                
      62 +layout(std430, set = 0, binding = 1) readonly buffer TimaertLigh
         +ts {                                                            
      63 +    uint          count;                                        
      64 +    uint          _pad0;                                        
      65 +    uint          _pad1;                                        
      66 +    uint          _pad2;                                        
      67 +    GpuPointLight lights[];                                     
      68 +} u_pointLights;                                                
      69 +                                                                
      70 +// Sum the diffuse contribution of every active point light at a
         + world-space                                                    
      71 +// surface point `worldPos` with (already-normalised) normal `N`
         +. Smooth                                                        
      72 +// radius-bounded falloff so a light fades cleanly to nothing at
         + its radius edge                                                
      73 +// — no hard cutoff seam, no negative light. Pixel-art friendly:
         + a mild                                                         
      74 +// quadratic edge softening keeps pools of light readable rather
         + than physically                                                
      75 +// exact. Callers without a meaningful per-pixel normal (sprite 
         +billboards) can                                                 
      76 +// pass a constant facing normal to get a flat, unit-lit glow in
         +stead.                                                          
      77 +vec3 point_lights(vec3 worldPos, vec3 N) {                      
      78 +    vec3 acc = vec3(0.0);                                       
      79 +    uint n = min(u_pointLights.count, uint(TIMAERT_MAX_POINT_LIG
         +HTS));                                                          
      80 +    for (uint i = 0u; i < n; ++i) {                             
      81 +        vec3  Lp     = u_pointLights.lights[i].posRadius.xyz;   
      82 +        float radius = max(u_pointLights.lights[i].posRadius.w, 
         +1e-3);                                                          
      83 +        vec3  Lcol   = u_pointLights.lights[i].colorRgbInt.rgb; 
      84 +        float gain   = u_pointLights.lights[i].colorRgbInt.w;   
      85 +        vec3  toL    = Lp - worldPos;                           
      86 +        float dist   = length(toL);                             
      87 +        float atten  = clamp(1.0 - dist / radius, 0.0, 1.0);    
      88 +        atten *= atten;                                   // sof
         +t quadratic edge                                                
      89 +        float ndl = max(dot(N, toL / max(dist, 1e-3)), 0.0);    
      90 +        acc += Lcol * (gain * atten * ndl);                     
      91 +    }                                                           
      92 +    return acc;                                                 
      93 +}                                                               
      94 +                                                                
      95  #endif

⏺ std430 check: C++ GpuLightBuffer is count+_pad[3] (16B header) then
  GpuLight{pos[4];color[4]} (32B) elements at offset 16. My GLSL block is 4×uint
  (16B) then GpuPointLight{vec4;vec4} (32B, 16-aligned) array at offset 16 —
  exact match.

  Now wire the additive term into the two consumers that have a real world
  position + normal. Point-light positions live in the same window-relative space
  as vWorld (the space used for sun/shadow math), not the absolute gWorld used
  only for procedural detail:

      68 +ecs::LightEmitter bolt_light(float radius,                      
      69 +                             std::uint8_t r, std::uint8_t g, std
         +::uint8_t b) {                                                  
      70 +    const float rf = float(r), gf = float(g), bf = float(b);    
      71 +    const float peak = std::max(rf, std::max(gf, bf));          
      72 +    const float inv = peak > 1.0f ? 1.0f / peak : 1.0f;         
      73 +    ecs::LightEmitter le{};                                     
      74 +    le.offX = 0.0f; le.offY = kBoltLightHeightM; le.offZ = 0.0f;
      75 +    le.r = rf * inv; le.g = gf * inv; le.b = bf * inv;          
      76 +    le.radius = kBoltLightBaseRadiusM + kBoltLightRadiusPerR * r
         +adius;                                                          
      77 +    le.intensity = kBoltLightIntensity;                         
      78 +    return le;

  Universality gap — point_lights() currently reaches terrain (mesh.frag) and
  structures (struct.frag), but not the three billboard passes (trees, NPCs,
  creatures) or water. So at night a fireball or the player's lantern lights the
  ground under a bandit but leaves the bandit himself pitch-black — visually
  incoherent.

  The billboard-class shaders (tree/npc/creature) already #include 
  "lighting.glsl" and already bind descriptor set 0 (which carries the light SSBO
  at binding 1, since their pipelines are created with shadowSetLayout_). So
  closing the gap is a pure shader edit — no C++, descriptor, or pipeline change
  — making it a genuinely minimal, universal, low-risk increment.

  One design point: a billboard is a camera-facing card with no meaningful
  per-pixel normal, so point_lights()' N·L term is wrong for it (a torch at an
  NPC's height would give N·L≈0 and leave them dark). The correct model is
  distance-only — the sprite analogue of the flat sunTerm that lit_surface()
  already uses for these passes. I'll add a point_lights_flat() sibling sharing
  one attenuation formula.

  Let me set up task tracking and implement:

⏺ Now implement Inc 6. First, add point_lights_flat() to lighting.glsl — a
  distance-only sibling for billboards, sharing the same attenuation formula as
  point_lights():

⏺ Update(shaders/lighting.glsl)

       74  // quadratic edge softening keeps pools of light readable rathe
           r than physically
       75  // exact. Callers without a meaningful per-pixel normal (sprite
            billboards) can
       76  // pass a constant facing normal to get a flat, unit-lit glow i
           nstead.
       77 +// Shared radius-bounded falloff so the surface form (point_lig
          +hts) and the flat                                              
       78 +// billboard form (point_lights_flat) fade IDENTICALLY — a torc
          +h pool reaches the                                             
       79 +// same distance whether it lands on the ground or on a creatur
          +e standing in it.                                              
       80 +float point_light_atten(float dist, float radius) {            
       81 +    float a = clamp(1.0 - dist / max(radius, 1e-3), 0.0, 1.0); 
       82 +    return a * a;                                         // so
          +ft quadratic edge                                              
       83 +}                                                              
       84 +                                                               
       85  vec3 point_lights(vec3 worldPos, vec3 N) {
       86      vec3 acc = vec3(0.0);
       87      uint n = min(u_pointLights.count, uint(TIMAERT_MAX_POINT_LI
           GHTS));
       88      for (uint i = 0u; i < n; ++i) {
       89          vec3  Lp     = u_pointLights.lights[i].posRadius.xyz;
       82 -        float radius = max(u_pointLights.lights[i].posRadius.w,
          - 1e-3);                                                        
       90 +        float radius = u_pointLights.lights[i].posRadius.w;    
       91          vec3  Lcol   = u_pointLights.lights[i].colorRgbInt.rgb;
       92          float gain   = u_pointLights.lights[i].colorRgbInt.w;
       93          vec3  toL    = Lp - worldPos;
       94          float dist   = length(toL);
       87 -        float atten  = clamp(1.0 - dist / radius, 0.0, 1.0);   
       88 -        atten *= atten;                                   // so
          -ft quadratic edge                                              
       95 +        float atten  = point_light_atten(dist, radius);        
       96          float ndl = max(dot(N, toL / max(dist, 1e-3)), 0.0);
       97          acc += Lcol * (gain * atten * ndl);
       98      }
       99      return acc;
      100  }
      101  
      102 +// Billboard (sprite) form of the point-light sum. A camera-fac
          +ing card has no                                                
      103 +// meaningful per-pixel normal, so applying the surface N·L ter
          +m above would be                                               
      104 +// wrong: a torch at chest height gives N·L≈0 and would leave t
          +he sprite dark                                                 
      105 +// while lighting the ground beneath it — visually incoherent. 
          +Instead we drop                                                
      106 +// N·L and use distance attenuation ALONE (the sprite analogue 
          +of the flat                                                    
      107 +// sunTerm lit_surface() already uses for billboards), so an ac
          +tor standing in a                                              
      108 +// pool of light glows with it as its ground does. Same attenua
          +tion curve, same                                               
      109 +// buffer, same inert-when-count==0 guarantee as point_lights()
          +.                                                              
      110 +vec3 point_lights_flat(vec3 worldPos) {                        
      111 +    vec3 acc = vec3(0.0);                                      
      112 +    uint n = min(u_pointLights.count, uint(TIMAERT_MAX_POINT_LI
          +GHTS));                                                        
      113 +    for (uint i = 0u; i < n; ++i) {                            
      114 +        vec3  Lp     = u_pointLights.lights[i].posRadius.xyz;  
      115 +        float radius = u_pointLights.lights[i].posRadius.w;    
      116 +        vec3  Lcol   = u_pointLights.lights[i].colorRgbInt.rgb;
      117 +        float gain   = u_pointLights.lights[i].colorRgbInt.w;  
      118 +        float dist   = length(Lp - worldPos);                  
      119 +        acc += Lcol * (gain * point_light_atten(dist, radius));
      120 +    }                                                          
      121 +    return acc;                                                
      122 +}                                                              
      123 +

      6877 +            // the 16 m lantern, so a normal frame never stage
           +s one in the light.                                           
      6878 +            // Deterministic: we spawn, then relocate the new 
           +creature to                                                   
      6879 +            // player + forward*dist (forward = (cos yaw, sin 
           +yaw), the same tile-XY                                        
      6880 +            // convention movement/aim use).                  
      6881 +            std::fprintf(stderr, "[smoke] action=light_probe_c
           +apture\n");                                                   
      6882 +            std::fflush(stderr);                              
      6883 +            if (!app.worldLoaded) {                           
      6884 +                smoke_fail(app, "light_probe_capture without w
           +orld");                                                       
      6885 +                break;                                        
      6886 +            }

      6888 +                smoke_fail(app, "light_probe_capture needs sub
           +world_enter first");                                          
      6889 +                break;                                        
      6890 +            }                                                 
      6891 +            // Which creature to probe with. Default wolf (a c
           +lear quadruped                                                
      6892 +            // silhouette); any global-table monster id works 
           +(bear, goblin, ...).                                          
      6893 +            const char* probe = std::getenv("TIMAERT_SMOKE_PRO
           +BE");                                                         
      6894 +            if (!probe || probe[0] == '\0') probe = "wolf";   
      6895 +            // Distance ahead, clamped inside the lantern radi
           +us (16 m) so the                                              
      6896 +            // actor sits in a bright part of the pool. Defaul
           +t 7 m.                                                        
      6897 +            float dist = 7.0f;                                
      6898 +            if (const char* pd = std::getenv("TIMAERT_SMOKE_PR
           +OBE_DIST")) {                                                 
      6899 +                dist = float(std::atof(pd));                  
      6900 +                if (dist < 1.0f) dist = 1.0f;                 
      6901 +                if (dist > 15.0f) dist = 15.0f;               
      6902 +            }                                                 
      6903 +            // Snapshot existing creature entities so we can i
           +dentify the new one.                                          
      6904 +            auto is_creature = [&](entt::entity e) {          
      6905 +                if (!app.ecs.reg.all_of<sm::ecs::Sprite, sm::e
           +cs::Position>(e))                                             
      6906 +                    return false;                             
      6907 +                if (app.ecs.reg.all_of<sm::ecs::PlayerTag>(e))
           + return false;                                                
      6908 +                return app.ecs.reg.get<sm::ecs::Sprite>(e).arc
           +hetype != 0xFF;                                               
      6909 +            };                                                
      6910 +            std::vector<entt::entity> before;                 
      6911 +            for (auto e : app.ecs.reg.view<sm::ecs::Sprite, sm
           +::ecs::Position>())                                           
      6912 +                if (is_creature(e)) before.push_back(e);      
      6913 +            const std::uint32_t seed =                        
      6914 +                app.gs.worldSeed ^ 0x9E3779B9u ^ std::uint32_t
           +(before.size());                                              
      6915 +            if (!app.subworld.spawn_hostile_npc(probe, probe, 
           +1, seed)) {                                                   
      6916 +                smoke_fail(app, "light_probe_capture spawn fai
           +led");                                                        
      6917 +                break;                                        
      6918 +            }                                                 
      6919 +            // Find the freshly-created creature (in the after
           +-set, not before).                                            
      6920 +            entt::entity probeE = entt::null;                 
      6921 +            {                                                 
      6922 +                std::vector<entt::entity> beforeSorted = befor
           +e;                                                            
      6923 +                std::sort(beforeSorted.begin(), beforeSorted.e
           +nd());                                                        
      6924 +                for (auto e : app.ecs.reg.view<sm::ecs::Sprite
           +, sm::ecs::Position>()) {                                     
      6925 +                    if (!is_creature(e)) continue;            
      6926 +                    if (!std::binary_search(beforeSorted.begin
           +(),                                                           
      6927 +                                            beforeSorted.end()
           +, e)) {                                                       
      6928 +                        probeE = e;                           
      6929 +                        break;                                
      6930 +                    }                                         
      6931 +                }                                             
      6932 +            }                                                 
      6933 +            if (probeE == entt::null) {

           +creature not found");                                         
      6935 +                break;                                        
      6936 +            }                                                 
      6937 +            // Relocate it to a fixed point directly ahead of 
           +the player, inside                                            
      6938 +            // the lantern pool. forward = (cos yaw, sin yaw) 
           +in tile space.                                                
      6939 +            const float yaw = app.subworld.cam_yaw();         
      6940 +            const float fx = app.subworld.player_x() + std::co
           +s(yaw) * dist;                                                
      6941 +            const float fy = app.subworld.player_y() + std::si
           +n(yaw) * dist;                                                
      6942 +            auto& ppos = app.ecs.reg.get<sm::ecs::Position>(pr
           +obeE);                                                        
      6943 +            ppos.x = fx;                                      
      6944 +            ppos.y = fy;                                      
      6945 +            std::fprintf(stderr,                              
      6946 +                         "[smoke] light_probe_capture probe=%s
           + dist=%.2f "

      6952 +            std::fflush(stderr);

           +hotographed lit,

      6955 +            app.smoke.capturePending = true;

  ⎿  Added 12 lines
      101    `atten = clamp(1 − d/radius, 0, 1)²`. The sum is **unshadowed
           ** (the sun
      102    shadow map does not gate it) and returns exactly `vec3(0)` wh
           en the count is
      103    zero, so the whole feature is provably inert until an emitter
            exists.
      104 +  - *Two forms, one curve.* Surfaces with a real normal (terrai
          +n, structures)                                                 
      105 +    use `point_lights()` with the `N·L` term above. Camera-faci
          +ng **billboards**                                              
      106 +    (trees, NPCs, creatures) have no meaningful per-pixel norma
          +l, so they use                                                 
      107 +    `point_lights_flat()` — the **same** buffer, gain and `poin
          +t_light_atten()`                                               
      108 +    curve but **distance attenuation alone** (no `N·L`). Droppi
          +ng `N·L` is                                                    
      109 +    deliberate: a chest-height torch gives `N·L ≈ 0` on an upri
          +ght card and would                                             
      110 +    leave an actor dark while lighting the ground at its feet —
          + visually                                                      
      111 +    incoherent. This mirrors how `lit_surface()` already feeds 
          +billboards a flat                                              
      112 +    `sunTerm` instead of a per-pixel one. Result: a tree, NPC o
          +r creature standing                                            
      113 +    in a torch / spell / lantern pool warms with it exactly as 
          +its ground does.                                               
      114 +    Both forms share `point_light_atten(dist, radius)` so a poo
          +l reaches the same                                             
      115 +    distance whether it lands on the floor or on a body standin
          +g in it.                                                       
      116    - *One universal source.* Any subworld entity may carry a `Li
           ghtEmitter`
      117      ([src/ecs/components.h](src/ecs/components.h)); the rendere
           r's
      118      `gather_point_lights()` packs **every** `view<Position, Lig
           htEmitter,

⏺ I now have the complete picture. The design is clear:

  Shader side: water.frag needs #include "lighting.glsl" (which declares the
  set-0/binding-1 SSBO). Water is a specular surface, so the right term is a
  per-light glint reflection (half-vector, like the existing sun/moon core
  highlight) — a torch near the shore throwing a shimmering colored reflection
  across the ripples. This is exactly the "reflections" the directive names. It
  must be additive and time-of-day independent (added after the ambient
  multiply), matching how every other pass treats point lights.
  
  C++ side: water pipeline needs shadowSetLayout_ passed to create_mesh, and the
  water draw must bind litSet at set 0.
  
  Harness: gpu_smoke3d builds its own pipelines from the same .spv — its water
  pipeline must mirror the set-0 binding or it'll break when water.frag starts
  referencing set 0. Let me check how the harness handles water.
  
  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

  the harness water pipeline creation and its water draw call to mirror the
  change precisely.

⏺ I now have the complete picture. The design for Inc 7:

  - lighting.glsl: add a third point-light response form —
  point_lights_spec(worldPos, N, V) — a half-vector specular glint (a colored
  reflection of each light source), reusing the shared point_light_atten curve
  and the same buffer. This joins the diffuse point_lights() and flat
  point_lights_flat() forms in the one universal file.
  - water.frag: #include "lighting.glsl", add the glint additively after the
  day/night ambient wash (so a torch reflection is time-of-day-independent,
  matching the universal contract).
  - vk_renderer_3d.cpp: pass shadowSetLayout_ to the water pipeline + bind litSet
  at set 0 in the water draw.
  - gpu_smoke3d.cpp: mirror both (pipeline layout + draw bind) per the
  shared-shader contract.
  
  Let me make the shader edits first.

⏺ Update(shaders/lighting.glsl)
  ⎿  Added 30 lines
      121      return acc;
      122  }
      123  
      124 +// Specular (mirror-glint) form of the point-light sum, for shi
          +ny surfaces — the                                              
      125 +// water waves today, and any future wet / polished / metal sur
          +face. Where                                                    
      126 +// point_lights() answers with a diffuse wash and point_lights_
          +flat() with a flat                                             
      127 +// sprite glow, a specular surface answers with a half-vector h
          +ighlight: a small                                              
      128 +// coloured reflection of the light SOURCE that rides the surfa
          +ce normal, so on                                               
      129 +// animated water it shimmers as a moving reflection of a torch
          + or spell rather                                               
      130 +// than a static tint. Tight bright core + a soft wider halo so
          + the reflection                                                
      131 +// reads as a shimmering pool, not a single hard pixel. Same bu
          +ffer, same                                                     
      132 +// radius-bounded attenuation, and the same additive inert-when
          +-count==0 contract                                             
      133 +// as the other two forms — a light reaches exactly as far on t
          +he water as its                                                
      134 +// diffuse pool does on the ground beside it. V is the (normali
          +sed) surface→eye                                               
      135 +// direction; N the (per-fragment, wave-perturbed) surface norm
          +al.                                                            
      136 +vec3 point_lights_spec(vec3 worldPos, vec3 N, vec3 V) {        
      137 +    vec3 acc = vec3(0.0);                                      
      138 +    uint n = min(u_pointLights.count, uint(TIMAERT_MAX_POINT_LI
          +GHTS));                                                        
      139 +    for (uint i = 0u; i < n; ++i) {                            
      140 +        vec3  Lp     = u_pointLights.lights[i].posRadius.xyz;  
      141 +        float radius = u_pointLights.lights[i].posRadius.w;    
      142 +        vec3  Lcol   = u_pointLights.lights[i].colorRgbInt.rgb;
      143 +        float gain   = u_pointLights.lights[i].colorRgbInt.w;  
      144 +        vec3  toL    = Lp - worldPos;                          
      145 +        float dist   = length(toL);                            
      146 +        vec3  H      = normalize(toL / max(dist, 1e-3) + V);   
      147 +        float nh     = max(dot(N, H), 0.0);                    
      148 +        float glint  = pow(nh, 90.0) * 1.2 + pow(nh, 14.0) * 0.
          +22;                                                            
      149 +        acc += Lcol * (gain * point_light_atten(dist, radius) *
          + glint);                                                       
      150 +    }                                                          
      151 +    return acc;                                                
      152 +}                                                              
      153 +                                                               
      154  #endif

  1. NPC torches — guards are ECS entities, table-driven via kNpcTypeDefs[],
  bounded count (worst case ~12 in a max city; typically 2-3), zero renderer
  change needed (the universal gather already picks up any
  Position+LightEmitter+SubworldTag). A new field on NpcTypeDef + a shared
  maybe_emplace_torch helper. Small, safe, certain.
  2. Lit windows — houses are pure instance data, not ECS entities; up to 380 
  houses vs the 32-light budget. Forces a separate emitter pass + nearest-N
  culling + budget raise. Larger, riskier — deferred.
  3. Particles/trails/magic FX — nothing exists, and critically no additive-blend
  pipeline. Everything net-new. Biggest gap, biggest payoff, but a
  multi-increment build.
  
  The disciplined sequencing: ship the ready, certain win (guard torches — a
  near-exact replay of my shipped player-lantern and spell-bolt-glow emitters,
  and the explicitly-planned "NEXT" in memory) as Inc 8, then pivot to the
  particle-system foundation as the next major track. Guard torches naturally
  completes the emitter arc before I move to the visually distinct particle work.

  One critical realization from the budget analysis: even settlement guards
  aren't provably under the 32-light cap — a 3×3 window with multiple dense
  cities could in principle exceed it, and gather_point_lights currently
  hard-breaks in arbitrary entt order, which could drop the player's own lantern.
  So the disciplined foundation is nearest-N culling first, then deploy emitters
  freely.

  Now I have everything for Inc 8 (nearest-N culling). The design:
  
  - Change gather_point_lights signature to accept the camera world position
  (vec3 camPos).
  - In the loop, instead of hard-breaking at 32: gather all candidate lights into
  a small local scratch, then if count > budget, partial-sort by squared
  distance to camPos and keep the nearest kSubworldMaxLights.
  - Player lantern sits at the camera → distance ≈ (offset only) → always in the
  nearest set.
  - When count ≤ budget: behavior must stay byte-identical to today (same
  entities, no reordering that changes anything observable — order in the SSBO
  doesn't affect the additive sum, but I'll preserve it anyway for a clean diff).
  
  Let me write the implementation. First the header signature:
  
  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

  have everything.

  Now let me design the culling carefully. The current gather writes directly
  into buf->lights[n] as it iterates. For nearest-N, I need to see all candidates
  before deciding which to keep. The cleanest approach that stays byte-identical
  when count ≤ budget:
  
  - Iterate the view, building each GpuLight into a local std::vector<GpuLight>
  (or fixed scratch) — same construction code as today.
  - If the vector size ≤ kSubworldMaxLights: copy all into buf->lights in
  iteration order (identical to today).
  - If > budget: std::nth_element by squared distance from camPos to partition
  the nearest kSubworldMaxLights, then copy those.
  
  Since the additive sum in the shader is order-independent, and when ≤32 I
  preserve iteration order exactly, sub-32 scenes are byte-identical. Let me
  write it. First, update the header declaration:

  the culling into a pure, testable free function in lighting.h (where GpuLight
  lives), so the renderer calls it and a standalone test exercises it with no
  Vulkan.

         +.

         +al to the

         +s.

      67 +//     distance from `camPos` (partitioned via nth_element, O(n)
         +; the tail order

         + light rides the

         +d never dropped                                                 
      70 +//     for a distant torch. The shader sum is order-independent 
         +⇒ partitioning                                                  
      71 +//     the survivors changes nothing visible.                   
      72 +//                                                              
      73 +// Pure and Vulkan-free so it is unit-testable on its own (see  
      74 +// point_light_cull_test); the renderer copies the surviving pre
         +fix into the                                                    
      75 +// mapped SSBO. `budget` is a parameter (not the constant) purel
         +y so the test can                                               
      76 +// exercise the overflow path at a small size.                  
      77 +inline void cull_nearest_lights(std::vector<GpuLight>& lights,  
      78 +                                const vec3& camPos,             
      79 +                                std::size_t budget) {           
      80 +    if (lights.size() <= budget) return;                        
      81 +    std::nth_element(                                           
      82 +        lights.begin(), lights.begin() + budget, lights.end(),  
      83 +        [&camPos](const GpuLight& a, const GpuLight& b) {       
      84 +            const float ax = a.pos[0] - camPos.x, ay = a.pos[1] 
         +- camPos.y,                                                     
      85 +                        az = a.pos[2] - camPos.z;               
      86 +            const float bx = b.pos[0] - camPos.x, by = b.pos[1] 
         +- camPos.y,                                                     
      87 +                        bz = b.pos[2] - camPos.z;               
      88 +            return (ax * ax + ay * ay + az * az)                
      89 +                 < (bx * bx + by * by + bz * bz);               
      90 +        });                                                     
      91 +    lights.resize(budget);                                      
      92 +}                                                               
      93 +                                                                
      94  struct LightParameters {
      95      vec3  sunDir;        // toward the active body: sun by day,
          moon (≈ -sun) by night
      96      vec3  sunColor;      // its radiance, day-intensity folded i
          n; the moon adds a

⏺ Build is green (linked OK, no -Werror on the game target). But I introduced 7
  -Wmissing-field-initializers warnings on the rows that don't set the light
  fields. The behavior is correct (omitted trailing members are value-initialized
  to 0 → no light), but the noise is mine to clean up.

  The most elegant fix that preserves the opt-in contract: give the light fields
  default member initializers in the struct. Since C++14 a class with default
  member initializers is still an aggregate, so the existing brace-init of all
  rows keeps working, constexpr usage is unaffected, and clang suppresses the
  warning for any row that omits them. It also makes "default = no light"
  explicit in the type itself:
  
⏺ Update(src/macro/npc.h)
  ⎿  Added 8 lines, removed 4 lines
      74      // strictly opt-in and costs nothing for the rest. Colour is
           linear RGB, the
      75      // offset seats the light on the body (metres), intensity is
           the scalar gain
      76      // — the same knobs the player lantern uses.
      77 -    float lightRadius;       // attenuation reach (m); 0 = no ca
         -rried light                                                     
      78 -    float lightIntensity;    // scalar gain                     
      79 -    float lightR, lightG, lightB;   // linear RGB radiance      
      80 -    float lightHeight;       // metres up from the feet the ligh
         -t is seated                                                     
      77 +    // Default-initialised to "no light" so every row that omits
         + them (all but                                                  
      78 +    // the ones that opt in) is dark and warning-free — a class 
         +with default                                                    
      79 +    // member initialisers is still an aggregate (C++14+), so th
         +e constexpr                                                     
      80 +    // brace-init of kNpcTypeDefs below is unaffected.

⏺ Baseline is green (timaert builds clean, no work to do). Task tracker set up.
  Four scouts are mapping in parallel:

  - Pipeline architecture — every VkPipeline's blend/depth/cull state,
  render-pass vs dynamic rendering, draw order, descriptor layout,
  pipeline-builder shape
  - Billboard path — instance format, camera-facing quad shaders, texture
  binding, how to reuse it for particles
  - Combat/spell ECS — existing spell-bolt/projectile/death hooks to hang FX off,
  and any TTL/lifetime component
  - Shader build — glslc pipeline, whether new shaders are auto-globbed, #include
  mechanism for lighting.glsl
  
  Design thinking while they run — the shape I'm converging on, to be confirmed
  against the real code:
  
  - Flat POD particle pool (not one ECS entity per particle) — data-oriented,
  scales to thousands cheaply. Emitters are ECS components (persistent, ride
  entities like a torch or moving bolt); individual particles are a transient CPU
  pool.
  - Universal ParticleEmitter POD + a preset table (kFxPresets[] indexed by an
  FxKind enum), mirroring the one-source-of-truth pattern of the
  monster/loot/NpcTypeDef tables the owner mandates. One POD models both
  continuous (rate) and burst (one-shot count) emission.
  - Lead with additive blending (SRC_ALPHA, ONE): additive is commutative →
  order-independent → no per-frame sorting needed, a major simplification, and
  it's exactly the magic-glow/FF aesthetic. Depth-test ON (terrain occludes),
  depth-write OFF.
  - Procedural radial spark in the fragment shader (smoothstep falloff) — no
  texture asset needed for v1, stays minimal and data-driven.
  - Verifiable with the exact Inc 9 methodology: spawn a burst at the probe,
  additive bloom is unmistakable in raw-pixel A/B.
  
  I'll hold for the scout reports before committing to the design, since the real
  code shape (dynamic rendering vs render pass, the billboard instance struct,
  which combat hooks exist) determines the minimal implementation.

⏺ Agent "Map billboard rendering path" finished · 3m 29s

⏺ Billboard path fully mapped. The template is clear:

  - Instance upload: copy the NPC dynamic pattern — device-local buffer sized
  once at init + per-frame vkCmdUpdateBuffer + transfer→vertex barrier (no
  per-frame alloc, no staging). Not the static tree path.
  - Quad: the 6-vertex gl_VertexIndex quad in billboard.vert:26-42, but centered
  (c.y ∈ {-0.5,0.5}) and using camera-up (add a camUp vec4 to BbPush, which
  already carries mvp+camRight) for full spherical billboarding — particles face
  the camera in all axes, unlike feet-anchored trees.
  - Draw slot: after creatures, at/around water (vk_renderer_3d.cpp:1740-1797),
  with blend=true, depthWrite=false, depthTest=true — terrain/creatures occlude
  particles, but particles don't occlude each other.
  - Additive → no sorting. The scout flagged that alpha-blended particles need
  back-to-front sorting; I'll sidestep that entirely by leading with additive
  blend (SRC_ALPHA, ONE), which is order-independent and the FF magic-glow look.
  Emissive frag (skip lit_surface, output color directly);
  sparks/fire/explosions/spell-trails all fit.
  - Shadow pass: skip it for particles (they don't cast shadows).
  
  Two scouts still out — the pipeline blend-state map (does create_mesh in
  vk_pipeline.cpp expose custom blend factors, or only opaque/alpha?) and the
  combat/spell ECS hooks (what death/impact/cast events exist to attach FX to).
  Both are load-bearing for the design, so I'll wait for them before writing
  code.

⏺ Agent "Map 3D renderer pipeline architecture" finished · 3m 46s

⏺ Agent "Map spell/projectile/combat ECS + current FX" finished · 3m 49s

⏺ All four scouts are back — I have a complete, precise map. Let me synthesize
  the critical findings before reading the exact code I'll touch.

  Foundation facts that shape the design:
  
  - Classic VkRenderPass, 1 subpass, color = swapchain B8G8R8A8_UNORM (8-bit LDR,
  no HDR float → additive saturates at 1.0, which is fine for pixel-art glow).
  - No additive blend exists. create_mesh (vk_pipeline.cpp:247-257) hardcodes
  straight-alpha; the blend bool only flips blendEnable. I must teach the helper
  additive — a trailing bool additive=false param leaves all 7 existing call
  - Draw slot: after creatures, before water (vk_renderer_3d.cpp:1769),

⏺ The renderer is fully mapped. Now the decisive question is where particles live
  and how the renderer sees them. Two candidate designs:

  1. Entity-per-particle — zero new plumbing (renderer already reads the ECS in
  prepare_frame/gather_point_lights), but per-frame entity churn and not
  unit-testable without Vulkan.
  2. Flat POD pool + pure sim — the textbook data-oriented design: transient VFX
  are not game entities, capacity is fixed/deterministic, and the sim is
  trivially unit-testable with zero Vulkan (matches the test-suite +
  airtight-verification mandate). Emitters stay universal ECS components; the
  pool just reads them.
  
  I'm choosing the flat POD pool — correctness and testability are the owner's
  only brake, and a pure sim I can prove in a standalone test beats entity churn
  I can't. The one cost is threading a const ParticleSystem* to the renderer; I
  own all the call sites. Let me verify them before committing the design.
  
  Read 2 files (ctrl+o to expand)

  subsystems, then passed to renderer3dVk_.record_main, is the natural home. Let
  me see the rest of tick() and the engine's record_main/prepare_frame wrappers.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The architecture is fully clear now. SubworldEngine owns renderer3dVk_, ecs_,
  cam_, elapsed_, and drives tick(dt) → prepare_frame → record_shadow →
  record_main. Critically, tick_playing_runtime (which calls subworld.tick) runs
  before prepare_frame in the same frame — so a CPU particle sim ticked in tick()
  is visible the same frame (no stale-frame issue for gameplay-driven spawns;
  only smoke-script spawns need the settle defer).

⏺ I have the complete architecture and all supporting APIs confirmed:
  - sm::Rng (xorshift32, deterministic) exists → the particle sim can be a pure,
  unit-testable function.

  vkCmdUpdateBuffer (NPC pattern).
  
  Design decided — universal, data-driven, testable:
  - Flat POD particle pool (src/sub/particles.{h,cpp}, mine/net-new) — not
  entity-per-particle. Fixed Particle pool_[2048], swap-remove compaction,
  sm::Rng member → the whole sim is Vulkan-free and provable in a standalone test
  (the owner's correctness brake).
  - FxKind enum + kFxPresets[] constexpr table — one source of truth for
  spark/blood/fire/ice/magic/ember bursts, mirroring the NpcTypeDef/monster-table
  pattern. No per-effect hardcoding at call sites.
  - Additive blend (SRC_ALPHA, ONE) → order-independent (no sorting) + the FF
  magic-glow look. Emissive frag (procedural radial spark, no texture asset for
  v1).
  - Draw slot: after creatures, before water; depthTest=on, depthWrite=off.
  Engine owns the sim, packs ParticleInstance[], feeds the renderer (renderer
  stays ignorant of the sim, like it takes instances).

      873 +void Renderer3DVk::stage_particles(VkCommandBuffer cmd, const v
          +oid* data,                                                     
      874 +                                   std::uint32_t count) {      
      875 +    particleCount_ = std::min(count, kMaxParticleInstances);   
      876 +    if (particleCount_ == 0 || data == nullptr) return;        
      877 +    // Same per-frame path as NPC/creature instances: overwrite
          + the device-local                                              
      878 +    // buffer in place, then barrier TRANSFER_WRITE → VERTEX_AT
          +TRIBUTE_READ.                                                  
      879 +    vkCmdUpdateBuffer(cmd, particleInstBuf_.buffer, 0,         
      880 +                      VkDeviceSize(particleCount_) * sizeof(Par
          +ticleInstance),                                                
      881 +                      data);                                   
      882 +    VkMemoryBarrier mb{};                                      
      883 +    mb.sType = VK_STRUCTURE_TYPE_MEMORY_BARRIER;               
      884 +    mb.srcAccessMask = VK_ACCESS_TRANSFER_WRITE_BIT;           
      885 +    mb.dstAccessMask = VK_ACCESS_VERTEX_ATTRIBUTE_READ_BIT;    
      886 +    vkCmdPipelineBarrier(cmd, VK_PIPELINE_STAGE_TRANSFER_BIT,  
      887 +                         VK_PIPELINE_STAGE_VERTEX_INPUT_BIT,   
      888 +                         0, 1, &mb, 0, nullptr, 0, nullptr);   
      889 +}

      489 +---                                                            
      490 +                                                               
      491 +## Particles / additive FX                                     
      492 +                                                               
      493 +Transient visual effects — spell trails, impact bursts, blood, 
          +dust, embers,                                                  
      494 +explosions — render as **additive billboards** in a pass drawn 
          +after all opaque                                               
      495 +geometry and creatures, immediately before water. The pass is *
          +*emissive**: it                                                
      496 +binds **no descriptor set** (no lighting, no shadow — the parti
          +cles *are* the                                                 
      497 +light), keeps depth-**test** on so terrain/trees/creatures occl
          +ude them, and                                                  
      498 +depth-**write off** + **additive blend** (`dstColorBlendFactor 
          += ONE`) so                                                     
      499 +overlapping cards accumulate into a glow with **no back-to-fron
          +t sort**. Additive                                             
      500 +blend is order-independent by construction, which is the whole 
          +reason the pool                                                
      501 +needs no per-frame depth sort. At the 8-bit LDR swapchain, dens
          +e overlap                                                      
      502 +saturates to white — exactly the white-hot-core-to-warm-halo lo
          +ok of pixel-art /                                              
      503 +Final-Fantasy magic FX.                                        
      504 +                                                               
      505 +**The pipeline flag.** `create_mesh(..., additive)` (the traili
          +ng bool on both                                                
      506 +overloads, [vk_pipeline.h](src/gpu/vk_pipeline.h)) is the *only
          +* renderer-side                                                
      507 +switch: `additive=true` flips the colour-blend `dst` factor fro
          +m                                                              
      508 +`ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` to `ONE`. Every other pass passes the def
          +ault `false` and                                               
      509 +is byte-for-byte unchanged. The particle pipeline is the sole c
          +aller today.                                                   
      510 +                                                               
      511 +**The sim is Vulkan-free and lives on the engine, not the rende
          +rer.** Transient                                               
      512 +VFX are deliberately **not** ECS entities — they are a flat POD
          + pool                                                          
      513 +(`Particle pool_[2048]`) advanced by a pure CPU integrator in  
      514 +[src/sub/particles.h](src/sub/particles.h) / `.cpp`. This keeps
          + the sim                                                       
      515 +**standalone-unit-testable** (`particle_sim_test` asserts table
          + ranges, emit                                                  
      516 +counts, lifecycle reaping, physics sign, pack envelope, and see
          +d determinism with                                             
      517 +zero GPU) — the project's correctness brake. The engine owns a 
          +`ParticleSystem`,                                              
      518 +ticks it in `SubworldEngine::tick(dt)` (pure integrate + reap, 
          +no ECS churn), and                                             
      519 +in `prepare_frame` packs the live pool into a reused scratch bu
          +ffer and hands it                                              
      520 +to `Renderer3DVk::stage_particles()`. Emitters stay **universal
          + ECS components**;                                             
      521 +the combat / spell ticks feed the pool, so there is no per-effe
          +ct hardcoding at                                               
      522 +the call sites.                                                
      523 +                                                               
      524 +**One table, no hardcoding.** A `FxKind` enum + a `constexpr kF
          +xPresets[]` table                                              
      525 +(mirrors the monster/loot/`NpcTypeDef` "one table" rule) is the
          + single source of                                              
      526 +truth for every effect's count, speed, gravity, lifetime, size 
          +envelope, drag,                                                
      527 +colour and spread. Adding an effect is one table row, not code 
          +at the spawn site.                                             
      528 +                                                               
      529 +**Upload path** mirrors the NPC dynamic-instance pattern exactl
          +y:                                                             
      530 +`stage_particles()` clamps the count to `kMaxParticleInstances`
          + (2048), then a                                                
      531 +per-frame `vkCmdUpdateBuffer` + `TRANSFER_WRITE → VERTEX_ATTRIB
          +UTE_READ` barrier                                              
      532 +into the device-local instance buffer. The 2048 ceiling is not 
          +arbitrary: at 32 B                                             
      533 +per `ParticleInstance` it is exactly the 64 KiB `vkCmdUpdateBuf
          +fer` per-call                                                  
      534 +maximum. A `static_assert` pins `sizeof(ParticleInstance) == 32
          +` and                                                          
      535 +`kMaxParticleInstances == ParticleSystem::kMaxParticles` so the
          + vertex-attribute                                              
      536 +layout and the pool size can never silently desync.            
      537 +                                                               
      538 +**The billboard.** [shaders/particle.vert](shaders/particle.ver
          +t) is a *centred,                                              
      539 +fully camera-facing* quad — it needs **both** camera axes (`cam
          +Right` **and**                                                 
      540 +`camUp` in `ParticlePush`), unlike the cylindrical tree billboa
          +rds that pivot on                                              
      541 +world-up, because a spark has no up. Three instanced attributes
          +: `vec3 pos`,                                                  
      542 +`float size`, `vec4 colour` (rgb + envelope alpha).            
      543 +[shaders/particle.frag](shaders/particle.frag) is a soft radial
          + spark: a                                                      
      544 +squared `1 − r` falloff from the quad centre, alpha-premultipli
          +ed, whitening the                                              
      545 +core (`mix(colour, white, fall·0.35)`) so dense cores read as w
          +hite-hot; it                                                   
      546 +`discard`s once alpha drops below a threshold.                 
      547 +                                                               
      548 +**The pass self-skips when the pool is empty** (`particleCount_
          + == 0`, the common                                             
      549 +case) → zero cost when nothing is emitting. This is why every n
          +on-FX scene today                                              
      550 +is unchanged.                                                  
      551 +                                                               
      552 +Verify it headless with `GPU_SMOKE_FX=1` (see §Frame capture): 
          +it stages a                                                    
      553 +standing additive burst over the cluster centre. Paired with `G
          +PU_SMOKE_NIGHT=1`                                              
      554 +it produces an unmistakable warm bloom against the dark forest;
          + the A/B proof                                                 
      555 +(FX on vs off, same camera) shows **only** brightening — zero p
          +ixels darken,                                                  
      556 +because additive can only add — with the core saturating from n
          +ear-black night                                                
      557 +ground to `(255,255,254)`. Midday shows **no blowout** (0 % nea
          +r-white pixels).                                               
      558 +

⏺ Now I have the full spell picture. Each bolt's Sprite tint encodes its element
  (fireball orange 0xFFCC00, ice white, magic violet 0xE0C0FF, lightning yellow).
  The elegant universal design crystallizes:

  - Trail: always SpellTrail, tinted by the bolt's own Sprite color (normalized
  exactly like bolt_light). One emit per bolt per tick.
  - Impact: archetype picked by the single existing blastRadius field — >0 → 
  FireBurst (explosions bloom upward), ==0 → MagicBurst (point-hits
  sparkle/float) — tinted by the same Sprite color, so lightning/energy/any
  future spell get correctly-colored bursts for free with zero new code.
  FireBurst scaled by blastRadius.
  
  Routing mirrors the existing SpellDamageLogFn idiom: a new SpellFxEmitFn
  callback keeps spell_effects.cpp particle/renderer-free; the engine-side impl
  does tile→world + FxKind choice. Let me confirm the conversion helpers and the
  existing pattern at engine.cpp:1978:
  
  Read 3 files (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Design locked. Inc B is clean and fully data-driven:

  - Trail: a new emit_streak() on ParticleSystem (pure, unit-testable, lives in
  my particles.h/.cpp) walks the bolt's per-tick movement segment and sheds
  SpellTrail motes ~every 1.5 m — framerate-independent, continuous even for 280
  m/s bolts.
  - Impact: burst shape chosen by the one physical field available (blastRadius >
  0 → FireBurst bloom, else MagicBurst pop), burst color always from the bolt's
  own Sprite tint (normalized identically to bolt_light()). So fireball→orange
  bloom, magic→violet pop, lightning→yellow pop, ice→white pop, and any future
  spell is correct for free. Zero per-spell branching.
  - Routing: a SpellFxEmitFn callback mirroring the existing SpellDamageLogFn
  idiom keeps spell_effects.cpp renderer/particle-free.

⏺ Update(src/sub/particles.cpp)
  ⎿  Added 24 lines
      112      return accum;
      113  }
      114  
      115 +void ParticleSystem::emit_streak(FxKind kind, vec3 a, vec3 b, f
          +loat spacingM,                                                 
      116 +                                 const vec3* tint, float scale)
          + {                                                             
      117 +    const FxPreset& fx = fx_preset(kind);                      
      118 +    const vec3 col = tint ? *tint : vec3{fx.r, fx.g, fx.b};    
      119 +    const vec3 d = b - a;                                      
      120 +    const float dist = length(d);                              
      121 +    // Degenerate segment (bolt barely moved) or no spacing: on
          +e mote at the head.                                            
      122 +    if (spacingM <= 0.0f || dist <= spacingM) {                
      123 +        spawn_one(fx, b, col, scale);                          
      124 +        return;                                                
      125 +    }                                                          
      126 +    // One mote per `spacingM` of travel, seeded at evenly inte
          +rpolated points                                                
      127 +    // from the tail toward the head so a fast bolt lays a cont
          +inuous streak                                                  
      128 +    // rather than a single clump at `b`. Cap the count defensi
          +vely so an absurd                                              
      129 +    // segment (e.g. a teleport) can never flood the pool in on
          +e call.                                                        
      130 +    int n = static_cast<int>(dist / spacingM);                 
      131 +    if (n > 64) n = 64;                                        
      132 +    const float inv = 1.0f / static_cast<float>(n);            
      133 +    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {                             
      134 +        const float t = static_cast<float>(i) * inv; // (0,1], 
          +head-inclusive                                                 
      135 +        spawn_one(fx, a + d * t, col, scale);                  
      136 +    }                                                          
      137 +}                                                              
      138 +                                                               
      139  void ParticleSystem::tick(float dt) {
      140      if (dt <= 0.0f) return;
      141      for (int i = 0; i < count_;) {
  ⎿  Found 10 new diagnostic issues in 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

      186 +void test_streak() {                                           
      187 +    // (a) Density is per-metre of travel, NOT per-call: a 10 m
          + segment lays ~5                                               
      188 +    // motes at 2 m spacing regardless of how the distance is s
          +plit across calls.                                             
      189 +    // One long hop and many short hops over the same path spaw
          +n the same count.

      191 +        ParticleSystem oneHop(1u);                             
      192 +        oneHop.emit_streak(FxKind::SpellTrail, {0, 0, 0}, {10.0
          +f, 0, 0}, 2.0f);                                               
      193 +        const int nOne = oneHop.alive();                       
      194 +                                                               
      195 +        ParticleSystem manyHops(1u);                           
      196 +        vec3 prev{0, 0, 0};                                    
      197 +        for (int i = 1; i <= 10; ++i) {

          + 2.0f);

      1058      return true;

      1061 +void SubworldEngine::spell_fx_emit_callback(void* user,       
      1062 +                                            SpellFxEvent event
           +,                                                             
      1063 +                                            std::uint32_t enti
           +ty,                                                           
      1064 +                                            float ax, float ay
           +,                                                             
      1065 +                                            float bx, float by
           +,                                                             
      1066 +                                            float blastRadius)
           + {                                                            
      1067 +    auto* engine = static_cast<SubworldEngine*>(user);        
      1068 +    if (!engine || !engine->ecs_) return;                     
      1069 +    auto& reg = engine->ecs_->reg;                            
      1070 +                                                              
      1071 +    // Colour every VFX from the bolt's OWN Sprite tint — the 
           +universal rule: a                                             
      1072 +    // bolt's colour drives both its trail and its impact (fir
           +eball warm, magic                                             
      1073 +    // violet, lightning gold, ice pale) with zero per-spell b
           +ranching. u8→linear                                           
      1074 +    // normalised so the brightest channel is 1.0, exactly lik
           +e the travelling                                              
      1075 +    // point light bolt_light() (registry.cpp) so wake, glow a
           +nd burst share one                                            
      1076 +    // hue. No Sprite (e.g. a bare meteor) ⇒ fall back to the 
           +preset colour.                                                
      1077 +    vec3 tint{};                                              
      1078 +    bool haveTint = false;                                    
      1079 +    const entt::entity e = entt::entity(entity);              
      1080 +    if (reg.valid(e)) {                                       
      1081 +        if (const auto* spr = reg.try_get<ecs::Sprite>(e)) {  
      1082 +            const float rf = float(spr->r);                   
      1083 +            const float gf = float(spr->g);                   
      1084 +            const float bf = float(spr->b);                   
      1085 +            const float peak = std::max(rf, std::max(gf, bf));
      1086 +            const float inv = peak > 1.0f ? 1.0f / peak : 1.0f
           +;                                                             
      1087 +            tint = {rf * inv, gf * inv, bf * inv};            
      1088 +            haveTint = true;                                  
      1089 +        }                                                     
      1090 +    }                                                         
      1091 +    const vec3* tintPtr = haveTint ? &tint : nullptr;         
      1092 +                                                              
      1093 +    // Seat the FX at the bolt's flight height (~1 m above gro
           +und, matching the                                             
      1094 +    // point-light offset) so the wake and burst ride where th
           +e bolt actually is.                                           
      1095 +    constexpr float kBoltFxHeightM = 1.0f;                    
      1096 +    auto& r3d = engine->renderer3dVk_;                        
      1097 +                                                              
      1098 +    if (event == SpellFxEvent::Trail) {                       
      1099 +        float wax = 0.0f, waz = 0.0f, wbx = 0.0f, wbz = 0.0f; 
      1100 +        Renderer3DVk::tile_to_world(ax, ay, wax, waz);        
      1101 +        Renderer3DVk::tile_to_world(bx, by, wbx, wbz);        
      1102 +        const float ya = r3d.sample_height_m(ax, ay) + kBoltFx
           +HeightM;                                                      
      1103 +        const float yb = r3d.sample_height_m(bx, by) + kBoltFx
           +HeightM;                                                      
      1104 +        // One mote per 1.5 m travelled ⇒ a continuous glowing
           + wake at any bolt                                             
      1105 +        // speed (distance-based, so 280 u/s and 400 u/s read 
           +the same density).                                            
      1106 +        constexpr float kTrailSpacingM = 1.5f;                
      1107 +        engine->particles_.emit_streak(FxKind::SpellTrail,    
      1108 +                                       vec3{wax, ya, waz}, vec
           +3{wbx, yb, wbz},                                              
      1109 +                                       kTrailSpacingM, tintPtr
           +);                                                            
      1110 +        return;                                               
      1111 +    }                                                         
      1112 +                                                              
      1113 +    // Impact: pick the burst archetype from the ONE physical 
           +field the bolt                                                
      1114 +    // carries — a positive blast radius means an explosive bl
           +oom (FireBurst),                                              
      1115 +    // otherwise a crisp arcane point-pop (MagicBurst). Scale 
           +grows (gently,                                                
      1116 +    // capped) with the blast so a fat fireball erupts bigger 
           +than a small charge.                                          
      1117 +    float wx = 0.0f, wz = 0.0f;                               
      1118 +    Renderer3DVk::tile_to_world(bx, by, wx, wz);              
      1119 +    const float y = r3d.sample_height_m(bx, by) + kBoltFxHeigh
           +tM;                                                           
      1120 +    if (blastRadius > 0.0f) {                                 
      1121 +        const float scale = std::clamp(1.0f + blastRadius * 0.
           +04f, 1.0f, 3.0f);                                             
      1122 +        engine->particles_.emit(FxKind::FireBurst, vec3{wx, y,
           + wz},                                                         
      1123 +                                tintPtr, scale);              
      1124 +    } else {                                                  
      1125 +        engine->particles_.emit(FxKind::MagicBurst, vec3{wx, y
           +, wz},                                                        
      1126 +                                tintPtr, 1.0f);               
      1127 +    }                                                         
      1128 +}

  ⎿  Added 51 lines
      556  because additive can only add — with the core saturating from n
           ear-black night
      557  ground to `(255,255,254)`. Midday shows **no blowout** (0 % nea
           r-white pixels).
      558  
      559 +### Inc B — spell-bolt trails + impact bursts                  
      560 +                                                               
      561 +The first gameplay emitter. Every flying spell bolt now sheds a
          + **glowing wake**                                              
      562 +as it travels and blooms an **impact burst** where it detonates
          + — both tinted by                                              
      563 +the bolt's *own* colour, with **zero per-spell branching**.    
      564 +                                                               
      565 +**Routing keeps `spell_effects.cpp` renderer-free.** The spell 
          +tick already used                                              
      566 +C-style `void* user` callbacks (`SpellDamageLogFn`, `SpellCanHi
          +tFn`) so the pure                                              
      567 +combat TU never sees engine/renderer types. Inc B adds one more
          + in the same shape:                                            
      568 +`SpellFxEmitFn(user, event, entity, ax,ay, bx,by, blastRadius)`
          +. The tick fires it                                            
      569 +at two points — a `Trail` event after each bolt-advance (with t
          +he tile-space                                                  
      570 +segment `prev→pos` the bolt just crossed) and an `Impact` event
          + at a hit or an                                                
      571 +explode-on-expiry (`ax,ay == bx,by`, the detonation point). `en
          +tity` is always the                                            
      572 +bolt, still valid at both call sites (fired *before* it is reap
          +ed), so the engine                                             
      573 +can read its `Sprite` for the colour. `spell_effects.cpp` stays
          + free of every                                                 
      574 +particle / renderer symbol; the engine's static `spell_fx_emit_
          +callback` is the                                               
      575 +only place the two worlds meet.                                
      576 +                                                               
      577 +**Colour comes from the bolt, not a lookup.** The callback read
          +s the bolt's                                                   
      578 +`Sprite{r,g,b}` (u8) and normalises it so the **peak channel → 
          +1.0**, hue                                                     
      579 +preserved — the *same* formula `bolt_light()` uses for the bolt
          +'s point-light                                                 
      580 +(registry.cpp), so wake, dynamic light and burst all share one 
          +hue. No `Sprite`                                               
      581 +(e.g. a bare meteor) ⇒ fall back to the `FxKind` preset colour.
          + A fireball trails                                             
      582 +warm orange, a frost bolt icy blue, an arcane charge violet — a
          +ll from data the                                               
      583 +bolt already carries.                                          
      584 +                                                               
      585 +**Archetype comes from one physical field.** The impact picks i
          +ts preset from the                                             
      586 +bolt's `blastRadius`: `> 0` ⇒ an explosive `FireBurst` bloom wh
          +ose scale grows                                                
      587 +(gently, capped `1..3×`) with the blast; `== 0` ⇒ a crisp `Magi
          +cBurst` point-pop.                                             
      588 +That is the *same* field the blast-damage path keys off, so the
          + visual can never                                              
      589 +disagree with the mechanics — a fat AoE fireball erupts big, a 
          +single-target charge                                           
      590 +pops small, with no per-spell table.                           
      591 +                                                               
      592 +**The trail is stateless and framerate-independent.** A transie
          +nt bolt lives only                                             
      593 +for the tick, so it carries no caller-owned accumulator. `emit_
          +streak(kind, a, b,                                             
      594 +spacingM, tint)` lays one mote every `spacingM` metres *of trav
          +el* (here 1.5 m),                                              
      595 +interpolating along `a→b` — density is per-metre, not per-frame
          +, so a 280 u/s and a                                           
      596 +400 u/s bolt read as the *same* continuous wake. It caps at 64 
          +motes per call                                                 
      597 +(a huge single-tick jump can't flood the pool) and degenerates 
          +to a single head                                               
      598 +mote when the segment is shorter than the spacing. The endpoint
          +s are converted                                                
      599 +tile→world and seated at the bolt's flight height (`sample_heig
          +ht_m + 1 m`, matching                                          
      600 +the point-light offset) so the wake rides exactly where the bol
          +t is.                                                          
      601 +                                                               
      602 +Verify it headless with `GPU_SMOKE_FX_TRAIL=1` (optionally `GPU
          +_SMOKE_FX_VIOLET=1`                                            
      603 +to recolour). The harness doesn't link `particles.cpp`; it hand
          +-stages an                                                     
      604 +Inc-B-shaped layout (a fading 16-mote line into a 4-ring head b
          +urst) through the                                              
      605 +byte-identical shipping particle pass, so the A/B isolates exac
          +tly the additive                                               
      606 +draw. Night A/B (trail on vs off, same camera): **zero** pixels
          + darken (additive                                              
      607 +can only add), and the mean *added* RGB is warm for fire (`R>G>
          +B`) vs violet for                                              
      608 +arcane (`B>R`) — the tints are measurably, and visibly, distinc
          +t.                                                             
      609 +

      1275 +    if (!ecs_) return;                                        
      1276 +    auto& reg = ecs_->reg;

           +dy, so droplets arc

           +{};
