  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The day crop is identical to night — the harness's default time-of-day is
  already dark, so that's not a true midday control (minor harness detail, not my
  concern right now). The critical finding stands and it's a real problem: on
  the additive/emissive pass, blood reads as a soft red glow (like a lamp) and
  dust saturates to a white-hot orb (like a flashbang). Neither reads as matter.
  This is the fundamental issue I flagged up front — additive can only add light,
  so dark blood can't be dark, and grey dust blows out.

  This is NOT a "100% certain improvement" yet — it looks like a bug. The honest
  root cause: blood and dust are matter (they occlude/absorb light), not emissive
  FX. They need an alpha-over blend, not additive. The emissive pass is correct
  for magic/fire/impacts/embers; matter needs its own material mode.
  
  Let me scope the renderer change. Let me look at how particles are staged and
  drawn:

  - Dust (lower-right) saturates to a white-hot orb — grey cards overlapping on
  additive push all channels to 255 = white flashbang, losing the tan hue.

     #version 450
     // Additive particle fragment (FX Inc A). Emissive: no lighting, no texture — a
     … +47 lines (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ This LOOK is decisive, and it confirms the risk I flagged at the outset: blood and dust are matter, but the particle pass is additive/emissive — so blood reads as a red glow (it can only add light, never darken) and dust saturates to a white
  orb (overlapping grey cards blow out to 255). That is not an improvement; it's a bug. Shipping it would violate the "100% certain improvement" gate.

  The honest root cause: the emissive/additive pass is correct for energy (magic, fire, impacts, embers — all shipped and verified) but wrong for matter. Matter needs alpha-over blending (it occludes/darkens). The correct, universal fix is a
  second material class on the FX system: a table-driven blend mode, partitioned pack, and an alpha-over pipeline alongside the additive one. This is squarely graphics/renderer/shader work and generalizes to any future matter particle (smoke,
  debris).

  Let me scope the exact touch points — the live Particle struct, spawn_one, and the draw code:

⏺ This LOOK is decisive and confirms the root-cause concern: blood and dust are matter, but the FX pass is additive/emissive. Additive can only add light, so blood glows red like a lamp (can't be dark) and dust saturates to a white orb (grey
  cards overlap to 255). Shipping this would fail the "100% certain improvement" gate — it's a bug, not an improvement.

  The correct, universal fix: give the FX system a second blend class — alpha-over (matter) alongside additive (energy) — selected by one table field. Energy FX (magic/fire/impacts/embers, all shipped) stay additive; matter (blood/dust, and any
  future smoke/debris) occludes correctly. The blend math works out cleanly: matter needs only create_mesh(additive=false) (which already exists) + a matter fragment shader (straight-alpha output, no white-hot lift). Let me confirm the vertex
  shader and the test's pack assertions before implementing:

⏺ This LOOK confirms the concern I raised at the start — and it's a genuine problem, not a tuning nit. On the additive/emissive pass, matter can't read as matter:
  - Blood (upper-left) is a soft red glow — like a red lamp, because dark-red on additive just emits dim red light. It can't be dark.
  - Dust (lower-right) saturates to a white-hot orb — grey cards overlapping on additive push all channels to 255 = white flashbang, losing the tan hue.

  Additive can only add light, so blood can't be dark and dust can't stay grey. This is not a "100% certain improvement" — it looks like a bug. Blood and dust are matter (they occlude/absorb), so they need alpha-over blending, unlike the
  correctly-emissive magic/fire/impact/ember effects.

⏺ This LOOK is decisive, and it confirms the risk I flagged at the outset: blood and dust are matter, but the particle pass is additive/emissive — so blood reads as a red glow (it can only add light, never darken) and dust saturates to a white
  orb (overlapping grey cards blow out to 255). That is not an improvement; it's a bug. Shipping it would violate the "100% certain improvement" gate.

  The honest root cause: the emissive/additive pass is correct for energy (magic, fire, impacts, embers — all shipped and verified) but wrong for matter. Matter needs alpha-over blending (it occludes/darkens). The correct, universal fix is a
  second material class on the FX system: a table-driven blend mode, partitioned pack, and an alpha-over pipeline alongside the additive one. This is squarely graphics/renderer/shader work and generalizes to any future matter particle (smoke,
  debris).
